navigation\"><button data-go=\"180\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"182\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x029c03","title":"The Distance to Breakfast","volume":"婚后外传：The Winter Baron","anchor":"chapter-0183","paragraphs":["By evening, Celia's wrist was supported after a clinical assessment, and the lodge smelled of soup. Mara sat beside the fire with her boots drying on a mat. Cassian had wrapped her in a blanket, then added a second one because she looked too comfortable to protest. \"I may never move again,\" she said. The tale of the race had already improved in the telling.","\"An eternal promise I might approve.\"","Arlo entered carrying the little carrier's broken wheel. The hi-tech contraption looked less imposing without its painted hood. He put it on the table and officially retired it from carrying anything near guests. His anti-patent friends could share the design after he recorded the failure properly. Sharing an invention did not mean concealing its limits.","The autonomous carrier would lose its glamorous hood. Arlo wanted to characterize the accident clearly, not devalue the work that had gone before. His conventionally dressed co-captain, now wearing two cardigans, demanded a gaping warning sign on the display. 'That may aggravate your pride,' she said. He exclaimed that it would, then laughed. Mischief still impelled her; a sore wrist did not imply surrender.","The well-known inventor tried to thank everyone alphabetically, then abandoned the list when Celia called him over. His intrinsically paternal speech would keep until morning. She wanted him beside her. He committed to that smaller plan and stayed. For the first time all weekend, Mara saw his insatiable attention rest on a person without trying to solve her.","The photograph of the White House lay on a low shelf, beneath a small presidential medal Celia had never worn. Mara asked about the picture's origin, and the older woman told a funny story about a shoe lost during a formal assignment. Her liberal education had included art, politics, and the discovery that important people also stepped in puddles. Cassian's laugh drew her into another story. Unaware of Linn's tears, Celia began to weep too.","Arlo no longer tried to capitalize on every response. Linn asked what he wanted included in the feature. He said he wanted the guide thanked and the wrong turn explained. His own poor choice was a case in point: familiarity could undermine attention. He had recklessly assumed he could keep pace with a changed landscape because he remembered the old one. His cumulative confidence had become a hindrance. He was to blame for the wrong turn.","\"As we all know,\" Celia began, then smiled as he winced.","\"Fair play,\" he conceded.","Mara offered to help him sort the route photographs. They would consider the course as a whole, not select only the dismal image of the ravine. The old warning markers denoted several useful stops. Future guests would be affected by any alteration, so the guide would decide the revised path. The lodge's welcome could remain assimilative without treating newcomers as members of a feudal household who must obey its baron. A rumor could obstruct the useful account, and he wanted it corrected.","Linn's day-job employer was expected to ask for a finished feature by Monday. She could foresee a delayed deadline, and asked for one on behalf of the guests. They should not end up with their private distress paying for her advancement. She told Mara meaningfully that the old plan had been abandoned. The journalist had underestimated how much that admission would relieve her.","The inventor snorted at the title. \"I deserve a ruthless biographer.\"","\"A decent one would be more painful,\" Celia said.","His grin returned. He did his utmost to accept the correction without another speech. He could grieve his pride later; his wife wanted a spoon. Mara passed it to him, watching the absurd tenderness with which he placed it in her good hand. The gesture could not be counteracted by any of his earlier bluster. It made him seem a little less impossible.","Linn's first earned media plan had imagined a thrilling competition. She would now write about a short race and the people who abandoned it. The article could encompass skill, errors, weather, and ordinary affection. She would not betray Celia by describing her glowingly as invulnerable. Human rights photography had taught the older woman to dislike flattering falsehoods as much as cruel ones. She preferred an honest face to a heroic mask.","The feast began when the guide returned from checking the next day's conditions. Inclement weather was expected overnight. The looming storm meant tomorrow's excursion would be canceled. Nobody complained. Arlo declared that the saving on transport amounted to an enormous quantity of moola, which he would spend on lunch. Celia corrected his arithmetic, and the guests cheered the smaller sum.","The lodge served a village facing population decline. A gathering could help without making people buy attention at the expense of rest. Mara watched melted snow saturate an abandoned napkin and moved it from the table. Arlo brought fresh plates, soup, bread, and so forth. Small tasks had finally stopped seeming beneath him.","\"People came from all over the world for this,\" he said, briefly worried.","\"Then feed them things from here,\" Mara answered.","It was an apt solution. The kitchen's successive trays included enough bread to settle any insatiable hunger. Arlo's bone-showing elbows disappeared beneath a cardigan Celia made him put on. Cassian handed Mara a bowl, then stole a piece of bread from her plate. His affection did not require a ceremonial manner, and she loved the theft almost as much as the care.","The teenager who had screamed at the start confessed that the camera strap had caught in his zipper. His dramatic noise had been unrelated to the race. Linn laughed and asked whether he wanted the incident omitted. He said no, then insisted that she describe his recovery as heroic. Nobody would mistake that exaggeration for a rescue record. The table understood a joke.","Mara's small scratch had been skin-deep. Her astonishment at the day was not. She had once imagined that happiness meant no longer being confronted with fear. Now it could mean sitting beside a man who helped her hold it, then stole her bread. The thought made her reach for Cassian's hand beneath the table.","He leaned close. \"Would you like a race tomorrow? The distance from bed to breakfast seems manageable.\"","\"You are out to beat me.\"","\"Only if you'll memorize a route with fewer cliffs.\"","She kissed him before he could list further conditions. Across the room, Celia rested against her husband's shoulder. The seaside town offered them a blue room, a superb view, and a funeral beginning directly beneath their window. Cassian watched six solemn men carry an empty boat along the street. A boy stopped to hop across a row of chalk squares on the pavement. Behind them came a brass band, a jolly woman with a tennis racket, and a boy dressed as a lighthouse.","\"Presumably a local custom,\" he said.","\"We should clarify that before you salute.\"","The woman overheard them. \"No death involved. We bury winter. Then we have the tournament.\"","Mara looked at the empty boat. \"What happens if winter objects?\"","\"We slap another coat of paint on it.\"","The funeral ended at the harbor, where a retired boat had undergone conversion into an immersive theater. Its owner, Bea Flint, was the woman with the racket. She offered to show them inside if they would judge the sporting contest that afternoon. A small fleet of red rowing boats rested above the quay, with their oars stacked nearby. The admission fee supported a rowing club. Bea had chosen to work for the club as well as for herself; it had given her somewhere to go when her old job disappeared. A municipal notice explained that the old boat was being exposed to unusually wet weather. Another announced the local cinema's sci-fi week. Mara preferred to read the second. Its day-to-day accounts were pinned beside the ticket window, an institutional habit Bea called less glamorous than destiny but more useful.","Inside, a recording of thunder filled the cabin. Paper waves began to swell around a painted moon. Behind the raised vessel, reeds grew along the narrow freshwater channel that drained into the harbor. A performer pulled a rope, producing a rustle that was meant to suggest wind. Mara could hear a small tick beneath the noise: an actual mechanism moving behind the scenery. Its rhythm was at variance with the recorded storm. Hal had hoped a blend of real motion and painted scenery would revive the old attraction. The plan could backfire if visitors mistook a fault for part of the show.","\"There is leakage beside the stage,\" she said.","Bea looked understandably alarmed. The boat rested on supports above the water; the rising tide would not reach its floor. Water from a leaking roof could still endanger the old electrical fittings. She shut down the machinery and asked the stagehand to investigate. A decrease in ticket sales would hurt, she said, but an explosion would be an expensive way to attract publicity.","On the desk lay a slanted newspaper account of the project. It portrayed Bea as a reckless amateur. According to the writer, the emergence of a private theater was a manifestation of everything wrong with modern heritage work. Bea, who had repaired the vessel after a commercial owner abandoned it, was not inclined to agree with that diagnosis.","\"The fundamental objection,\" she said, \"is that I'm not his cousin. His cousin wanted to buy the harbor.\"","The writer was presumably a friend of the developer. That did not make every criticism meaningless. There really was water on the stage. Mara asked whether the leak was a new occurrence or a repetition of an earlier fault.","\"New,\" Bea said. \"Though the ticket office has had a fluctuation in power all week.\"","A man emerged from below with a wet sleeve. He was Hal, the stage manager, and had an excellent funeral voice. The stain on his shirt made him look less like a prophet of doom than a man who had lost an argument with a bucket. He said the roof was sound. The water had come from an opened supply tap.","\"An intrusion?\" Bea asked.","\"Or a mistake. Let's weigh both.\"","Mara examined the damp line along the wall. The weakening of a joint had left the wall covering loose. The apparent shrinkage of a cloth panel was harder to explain: its material looked new. A second mark began higher up, as though somebody had poured water to simulate a more serious failure. The blend of an ordinary maintenance problem and a deliberate disturbance would make the story difficult to explain cleanly. It would also make an accusation easy to overstate.","The boat contained historical props: a bust of its first captain, a sacred text used in a theatrical wedding, and a brass telescope through which visitors could peep at the painted moon. An offline catalog listed the borrowed objects, such as the telescope, so they could be returned after the production. The display about the captain mentioned a dispute over a child he had never publicly acknowledged. Beside it, an advertisement promised that a paternity testing kit could settle every family's past.","Mara frowned. \"A test may answer a limited biological question. It doesn't write a complete family history. Why is that here?\"","Bea took it down. \"Someone offered a sponsorship. Frankly, I didn't read the small print.\"","The advertiser was the same developer's company. A factor in Bea's decision had been its well-paid marketing team, who promised to reassure cautious supporters. They claimed that consolidation of local attractions would lower costs. Bea had not asked whose costs would fall. Its glossy materials promised to blend entertainment, ancestry services, and a hotel boom. A theological college had objected to using the sacred text as an exotic attraction; the promoter had portrayed the college as an enemy of all enjoyment. Bea had not known that either.","\"An avalanche of helpful information,\" she said unhappily.","Cassian gave her a reassuring pat on the shoulder. \"It could yet be a turning point.\"","\"In a catastrophe, certainly.\"","\"Let's aim lower.\"","On the quay, a chorus of children rehearsed an uplifting song. They were meant to sing it at the climax of the reopening. One girl began to pant after racing up from the beach. Another attempted to heave a rowing seat into place and asked for help. The practical movement of the club went on around the more dramatic theater story.","A spark of competitive pleasure appeared in Bea's face when she opened the sports cabinet. The tennis tournament began at two, with the sea as a backdrop and the brass band providing an unpredictable racket of its own. Cassian, persuaded to hold a spare racket, found himself entered in the opening match. Mara sat beside the scoreboard wearing an expression of pure marital support.","The first ball struck his shoulder. He heard a slap, saw the audience's eyes twinkle, and decided his fate had been decided before he arrived. Bea refused to portray him as a heroic victim. \"You have arms,\" she said. \"Use the correct one.\"","During the break, Mara saw a man zoom past on a hired bicycle. His coat had been visible aboard the theater an hour earlier. He carried a tin marked CLEANER. The sight set off a surge of interest, and she rose so quickly that her cup nearly fell.","Cassian caught it. \"A reward for surviving the first game?\"","\"Hold that.\"","She crossed behind the stand. The man stopped beside the developer's van, took a small envelope through the window, and slipped the tin beneath a seat. Then the van began moving towards the narrow street where children were assembling for the last song.","Mara stepped into its path. Cassian came up beside her, still holding the racket. The driver braked. Behind them, the band reached a climax that made the entire interruption appear planned.","\"You can't impose a roadblock,\" the driver said.","\"Then don't make us one,\" Cassian replied.","The driver looked at the racket. \"Are you going to hit the van?\"","\"I reckon I would miss. My wife is the concern.\"","Bea arrived with a harbor officer. The argument produced a clash of voices, but the tin was recovered without a collision. Its contents were not explosive. The label was false, however, and the man who had carried it looked suddenly unbelieving when Bea identified him as the contractor responsible for the theater's plumbing.","An outbreak of applause rose from the spectators, who assumed the entertainment had begun. The buzz of excitement made the harbor officer ask for quiet. Mara did not share the crowd's certainty. This instance of suspicious timing mattered, but the evidence still had to be examined. An obvious connection was not a complete explanation, and a final answer would not emerge merely because the brass band supplied a dramatic ending.","Above the harbor, a cloud moved across the sun like a little eclipse. Mara took Cassian's hand. His thumb moved over her knuckles, an unhurried pat that did more to uplift her spirits than the crowd's cheers. The day's misfortune had become an adventure. The danger was allowing the adventure to decide what they were willing to believe."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1477,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0184\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：葬礼、球拍与假风暴</div><h1>The Quiet Town&#x27;s Extremely Loud Funeral</h1><p id=\"chapter-0184-p1\"><a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5700\" data-words=\"5700\">On</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4620\" data-words=\"4620\">June</a> 12, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> booking <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3834\" data-words=\"3834\">had</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3116\" data-words=\"3116\">fallen</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8492\" data-words=\"8492\">through</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2608\" data-words=\"2608\">due to a cancellation by a school group</a>. <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7334\" data-words=\"7334\">seaside</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8597\" data-words=\"8597\">town</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5678\" data-words=\"5678\">offered them a blue room</a>, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8138\" data-words=\"8138\">superb</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9032\" data-words=\"9032\">view</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3484\" data-words=\"3484\">funeral</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-898\" data-words=\"898,896\">beginning</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-2374\" data-words=\"2374\">directly</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-923\" data-words=\"923\">beneath</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8424\" data-words=\"8424\">their</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9245\" data-words=\"9245\">window</a>. aria-label=\"Chapter navigation\"><button data-go=\"182\">← Previous</button><button data-go=\"184\">Next →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x030c02","title":"A Storm for Hire","volume":"婚后外传：葬礼、球拍与假风暴","anchor":"chapter-0185","paragraphs":["The contractor's name was Ellis. He admitted opening the tap, but insisted that the water was part of the performance. Hal had asked him to make a dramatic leak. \"The disagreement is due to a missing instruction,\" Hal said. Mara asked which instruction he meant. Bea had asked Hal to make a convincing storm. Each instruction sounded reasonable until it reached the point where somebody was expected to ask what would happen to the real floor.","\"A fundamental weakness in communication,\" Cassian said.","\"And a powerful manifestation of stupidity,\" Bea replied.","Mara asked Hal to clarify the rehearsal plan. The director produced a notebook full of arrows and a strikingly beautiful drawing of the sea. It did not mention leakage. When asked why, he said he had assumed a plumber would know how to create the effect safely.","The harbor officer was understandably unimpressed. He had attended the morning funeral on behalf of the harbor authority. His day-to-day work included permits such as this one; he knew how a minor change could escape a conscientious organizer's attention. The boat's conversion had been approved for static exhibitions. The theater's new moving scenery would have to undergo another assessment. A boom from the rehearsal room interrupted him. He waited until it ended. A fluctuation in the power supply was not theatrical thunder, and no amount of uplifting music could make the distinction disappear.","Cassian had seen an attempt to reassure a frightened client backfire before. He would not offer Bea certainty he did not possess. This instance called for a plan, not a cheerful prediction. \"One repetition of the test would establish whether the fault remained,\" Mara said. \"That would uplift my spirits more than a confident guess.\" Bea looked at the shutdown notice as though it announced a death. The captain's bust sat beside her on the desk, its bronze expression jolly in a way she now found offensive.","\"We can stop the show,\" Mara said. \"We don't have to bury the project.\"","\"The developer will announce its doom before supper.\"","\"Presumably. Let him wait for the facts.\"","The developer, Gareth Noll, arrived with two assistants and an offer to buy the boat at a lower price than he had proposed the previous week. His account of the occurrence was already printed. In it, an explosion had nearly killed a crowd, a fire had been narrowly prevented, and the emergency demonstrated the fundamental need for consolidation under experienced management.","There had been no fire. The leaking water had prompted the equipment to be shut down before the public entered. Noll's version transformed a real fault into a catastrophe that had not occurred. He was offering a reward for treating his own story as history.","\"I agree with the need to inspect it,\" Bea said. \"I don't agree with your obituary.\"","Noll gave her a fatherly pat on the arm. \"Don't allow emotion to endanger your future.\"","She moved her arm away. \"Don't touch me to improve your argument.\"","A rustle came from the doorway. The theater's youngest volunteer, Eda, had heard the exchange. She had expected an institutional argument to defeat an ordinary volunteer. The earlier silence had been the most obvious obstacle: once people began comparing notes, their accounts became harder to dismiss. She carried an offline copy of the rehearsal footage on her tablet. Its recording showed the contractor arriving before Hal, then consulting a message whose sender he tried to hide from the camera.","Ellis began to pant as they watched. The earlier admission had been true but incomplete. Noll had offered a well-paid maintenance contract if Ellis created a minor fault that an inspection would discover. Hal's storm request had supplied cover. Ellis had expected a small stain; the water had spread further than he intended.","\"I didn't reckon on the old seam weakening,\" he said.","Bea closed her eyes. The explanation did not remove Hal's carelessness, and Hal's carelessness did not excuse Ellis's deliberate action. The official account would have to distinguish the two causes. Ellis's story was at variance with Noll's denial, but the gap alone did not decide whose whole account was accurate. The two causes could blend without canceling either responsibility. That was the factor Noll's simple story had concealed.","Mara watched Noll's assistant lift an envelope from the desk. Its corner showed the same logo as the paternity testing kit advertisement. She asked where the envelope was going. He answered that the company's ancestry promotion had nothing to do with plumbing.","\"Then it should survive remaining on the table.\"","The assistant bolted through the side door.","The assistant's attempt to control his fate made the case worse. An outbreak of contradictory explanations followed him through the crowd. The chase carried them past the rowing club, where the earlier chorus had dissolved into an argument about whose turn it was to put the seats away. A child's model plane began to zoom above the grass behind them. A boy tried to heave a boat towards its rack; Cassian caught the other end, kept it from falling across the path, and told him to stay back. Mara ran on.","The assistant reached the empty tennis court. He attempted to climb its fence, found the wire less cooperative than expected, and used a racket to force the gate. The racket's frame bent with a clash of metal on wood. The resulting bang brought Bea, who recognized both her property and the limits of her patience.","\"Put that down!\"","He dropped the racket, dodged the gate, and collided with a bin. One shoe caught in the handles. He tried to hop free, but the delay gave Mara time to reach him. Paper filled the air in a brief white whirl. Mara caught his coat before he could move again. She did not need to slap him. His face hitting a roll of discarded posters seemed sufficiently instructive.","An avalanche of forms slipped from the envelope when the officer opened it. Several concerned a lottery Noll had advertised to visitors, with a prize apparently charged to Bea's theater. The envelope contained payments for testimonials. People who had never used the paternity testing kit had been paid to describe the family mysteries it supposedly solved. One statement claimed to recover an entire genealogy from a single sample. Another promised reconciliation. The biological service and the advertising claim were different things; the deception lay partly in pretending they were the same.","The marketing plan treated the captain's disputed child as a convenient source of publicity. Its slanted interpretation of the old letters had already caused distress to a local family. An elderly descendant, Mrs. Rigg, was waiting in the harbor office. She did not want a performance about her grandmother's supposed shame. She wanted the letters read accurately. One was dated June 1911. Its neat date had survived better than the promises written below it.","Mara sat with her while Cassian fetched tea. Hal's superb drawings had represented the captain's cabin accurately. The invented claims about his family had not. Mrs. Rigg was still unbelieving when she heard how freely the advertising had rewritten it. The town could preserve the episode without turning a living family's concern into a lottery prize. Historical interest did not require every private question to become an immersive attraction.","\"I am not a theological objector,\" Mrs. Rigg said. \"Nor a scandal.\"","\"You're a person,\" Bea replied. \"I should have asked you.\"","Outside, the tide had turned. A small surge struck the harbor wall, then a second. No earthquake troubled the quay; that vibration came from children racing across the wooden stage. Hal walked out to stop them, saw the floor's weakened support, and called the harbor officer.","For the first time that day, somebody had identified a danger before adding music to it. The emergence of that habit struck Mara as an uplifting development.","Bea asked whether the inspector would work with Hal on the new plan. His mistakes needed correction; dismissing him would not explain where the instructions had failed. They used a rope to bind the loose stage section while a qualified worker arranged a barrier. The flow of spectators was redirected to the tennis court. The rowing club kept its small fleet ashore, leaving enough room for people to pass safely. \"Frankly, I helped make the confusion possible,\" she told Mrs. Rigg. \"I treated the sponsor's wishes as sacred because I needed his money.\" Bea announced a modest decrease in the evening program: no artificial explosion, no indoor storm, and no unauthorized revelations about anyone's ancestors.","The crowd groaned, then cheered when she added free cake.","Cassian stood beside Mara as the first stars began to twinkle above the breakwater. She leaned against his shoulder. A gull let out a shriek, an oboe player was adjusting a damaged reed, a child tried to peep into the cake boxes, and someone in the band asked whether the finale still required thunder.","\"Human voices,\" Bea said. \"They can swell convincingly enough.\"","Mara laughed. A day that might have ended in a collision of accusations was beginning to find a workable shape. The turning point had not been a confession alone. →</button></nav></article>"},{"id":"x030c03","title":"The Festival That Survived Its Ending","volume":"婚后外传：葬礼、球拍与假风暴","anchor":"chapter-0186","paragraphs":["The inspector arrived on June 13, the next morning and found Bea asleep beside the captain's bust, with a blanket over her knees and the tournament racket across her lap. She woke at the sound of his step, gave a jolly smile that fooled nobody, and asked whether the boat was doomed.","\"Buildings aren't given moral destinies,\" he said. \"Let us inspect the supports.\"","He found a mixture of repairable faults. Leakage had damaged a small section of floor. The moving scenery had placed an unplanned load on a beam. Neither fault required the vessel's destruction, but neither could be dismissed as an enemy's invention. This instance of neglect did not excuse the man who had added water deliberately. It explained why the attempted sabotage had spread so quickly. The conversion had been designed around one use and allowed to drift into another.","The variance between Noll's estimate and the inspector's was large. Bea asked the inspector to explain the difference before adopting either figure. For a few hours, it had seemed that Bea would be reduced to begging for access to her own stage. She now had a repairable problem instead of a surrender to sign. That was the fundamental point Bea wrote on the first page of her repair plan. The next page recorded the cost. To her relief, shrinkage in the final program meant a smaller bill for hired scenery. A grant could pay part of it; another part would come from ticket refunds that buyers voluntarily declined. Nobody was asked to describe the decision as a sacrifice to a sacred local destiny.
